Why this is the pick

2-leg parlay at -109: Ohio State Buckeyes to win outright + Penn State Nittany Lions +4.5. Each leg on its own is priced too short to be worth backing, so they are combined to get the price to -109. The model puts the joint chance of all legs landing at 54%, against 52% implied by the price — that gap is the reason this qualifies.

Michigan State Spartans @ Ohio State Buckeyes — Ohio State Buckeyes to win outright. Ohio State Buckeyes rates 19.2 points stronger on power ratings; the biggest matchup edge is michigan state spartans running vs ohio state buckeyes run defense (a clear edge); the model wants 1.7 more points on Michigan State Spartans than the market prices. Both sides are starting their usual quarterbacks with no notable injury gap.

Michigan Wolverines @ Penn State Nittany Lions — Penn State Nittany Lions +4.5. Michigan Wolverines rates 4.7 points stronger on power ratings; the biggest matchup edge is penn state nittany lions running vs michigan wolverines run defense (essentially even). Both sides are starting their usual quarterbacks with no notable injury gap.

By the model's own numbers this returns +0.04 units per unit staked over the long run. That is the model's estimate, not a guarantee — the tracker records what actually happened.

Parlay board

How the board is built These are the most confident moneylines left after Pixel’s Pick takes its legs, stacked in confidence order until the combined price reaches +100 or better. No game appears twice, so each ticket is genuinely different rather than a reshuffle of the same games. Short favourites are worth little on their own; combining them is what turns them into a payout worth the risk. It does not turn them into an edge — every leg still carries its own vig, and the tracker records how these actually land.

Locked (). This is the pick of record: it will not change and it is what the tracker grades.

How the model sees it playing out

The power ratings see two closely matched teams, with only 0.1 points between them on raw strength. Overall, Western Michigan Broncos is the 104th-rated team in the league (a real weakness) and Central Michigan Chippewas is 105th (a real weakness). Western Michigan Broncos then adds the home-field bump the model fits at 3.3 points. Rolling that together, the engine projects Western Michigan Broncos by 2.5 and gives them a 56% chance to win outright.

On how it plays out: neither side owns a clear schematic edge — the unit ratings are close enough that field position and turnovers likely decide it. Expect a game that stays within one score into the fourth quarter, where late possessions carry outsized weight.

The market has this at Western Michigan Broncos -3.5. The model and the market are essentially in agreement here, which usually means no edge worth chasing.

One caution: the projection sits near 3, a key number in football margins. Games cluster on it, so small errors in the estimate flip outcomes against the spread more often than the point difference suggests.
